### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0061:c001 · PDF 61**

3.3.1. What is a Stage? — Dividing an Architecture Project into Stages

**G188:p0061:c002 · PDF 61**

PRINCE2 advocates dividing a project into manageable and controllable stages. PMBOK says that stages[7] can be broken down “by functional or partial objectives, intermediate results or deliverables, specific milestones within the overall scope of work, or financial availability”. Stages are generally time-bound, with a start and ending or control point.

**G188:p0061:c003 · PDF 61**

What stages should the Architecture Project be split into? The TOGAF Standard recommends an iterative approach in the ADM.[8] It suggests use of architecture development iterations that cycle through Phases B to F to deliver architecture. In such a case, each architecture development iteration would be executed as a separate stage.

**G188:p0061:c004 · PDF 61**

In general, a few options for how to split an Architecture Project into stages can be considered, depending on the project goal. Here are a few frequently used approaches:

**G188:p0061:c005 · PDF 61**

• Splitting the baseline architecture definition, target architecture definition, and transition planning into separate stages (note that the TOGAF Standard advocates both baseline-first and target-first approaches, depending on context[9])

**G188:p0061:c006 · PDF 61**

• Splitting into iterations based on a level of detail (e.g., the first iteration delivers a high-level outline that is detailed in the next iterations)

**G188:p0061:c007 · PDF 61**

• Splitting into iterations based on a level of consultation (e.g., the first iteration delivers an internal Enterprise Architecture draft, refined in the next iterations through consultation with groups of stakeholders)

**G188:p0061:c008 · PDF 61**

• Using a sequence of ADM phases, where each stage includes a single phase, or a number of phases (e.g., Architecture Vision, Business/IS/Technology Architecture, Opportunities & Solution/Migration Planning)

**G188:p0061:c009 · PDF 61**

• Single stage approach; i.e., the whole Architecture Project is executed as a single stage

**G188:p0061:c010 · PDF 61**

• A mix of these

**G188:p0061:c011 · PDF 61**

The approach should be chosen carefully to ensure it meets the Architecture Project goal and constraints such as time and budget. The TOGAF advice on choosing the right approach can be found in the TOGAF Standard — Applying the ADM: Applying Iteration to the ADM.

**G188:p0061:c012 · PDF 61**

Example 3-27

**G188:p0061:c013 · PDF 61**

In Project B, a complex business process is to be analyzed and optimized. The process scope is not fully understood, but it is known that three business units play key roles in the process, each of them divided into five subunits on average. After the process is analyzed, the team is going to model to be state for the process. Then, it is going to define and prioritize initiatives that will move the company to the target state.

**G188:p0062:c000 · PDF 62**

The architect leading the project decides on an approach based on splitting the baseline architecture definition, target architecture definition, and transition planning. He also decides to work on the baseline iteratively, with the first iteration goal to understand the whole process and plan detailed work. His plan includes the following stages:

**G188:p0062:c001 · PDF 62**

1. Process Overview (2 weeks) — preliminary analysis and general business process views definition, based on existing documentation and meetings with business unit leaders; approach and schedule confirmation based on process overview.

**G188:p0062:c002 · PDF 62**

2. Process Analysis (5 weeks) — thorough workshops with each of the subunits to define detailed process views including collaboration with other departments and external organizations, and IT systems support (15 workshops; 5 workshops per week); summary meetings with business unit leaders; process models review with subunits.

**G188:p0062:c003 · PDF 62**

3. Process Modeling (4 weeks) — target process modeling; new and changed logical IT components definition; target model review with subunits and business unit leaders.

**G188:p0062:c004 · PDF 62**

4. Transition Planning (3 weeks) — initiatives definition, prioritization, and roadmap preparation.

**G188:p0062:c005 · PDF 62**

Note that Stage 1 can be mapped to the ADM Phase A, Stages 2 and 3 cover activities from the ADM Phases B and C, and Stage 4 includes activities from the ADM Phases E and F.

</details>

<a id="3.3.2"></a>
## 规划一个阶段

原文：3.3.2. Planning a Stage。PDF 物理页：[62, 63, 64]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

按 PRINCE2，阶段应在其开始前规划，即在前一阶段结束时规划。这样可以在阶段规划中利用项目早期成果、风险与经验教训，也使任务从阶段开始即可执行与监控。通常，阶段边界已在项目计划中设定（见 3.1 节），因此在规划阶段时我们已知：

- 该阶段要交付哪些产品，即哪些视图；
- 期望达到何种详细程度；
- 哪些架构要素在范围内，如分段架构的流程和/或应用清单；
- 时间框架；
- 要完成的关键任务；
- 分配的资源。

若上述内容不清晰，使用 3.2 节所述技术加以确定。

视总体项目计划的详细程度，阶段计划可能已包含其中（即详细程度足以开展工作），也可能需要为该阶段编制更详细的计划。

PRINCE2 建议逐阶段规划工作（见 PRINCE2, §2.4）。在我们看来，这种方式适用于多数架构项目。实用的做法是有一份总体项目计划，清晰界定各阶段及其边界，但不必过深——它只应勾勒各阶段内的主要活动及其时间框架；因此，每个阶段开始前都须编制详细的阶段计划。

编制阶段计划可遵循 3.2 节关于项目计划的指南。方法相同——定义活动、排序、估算工作量、排程；但工作量与进度应保持在项目计划设定的阶段边界之内。

> **示例 3-28**
>
> 在项目 B 中，流程概览阶段之后，项目经理编制了下一阶段（流程分析）的计划。
>
> 项目经理的工作基于此前编制的项目计划，该计划勾勒了阶段进度：
>
> - 三（3）周用于与子单元召开工作坊
> - 一（1）周用于分析汇总与内部评审
> - 一（1）周用于子单元评审分析
> - 一（1）周用于处理评审中发现的问题
>
> 上一阶段（流程概览）还收集了以下附加信息：
>
> - 有 13 个子单元在物流流程执行中发挥重要作用，另有 3 个子单元起支持作用
> - 已为每个子单元确定联系人
>
> 基于这些输入，项目经理把阶段计划细化到天：
>
> - 将召开 13 次工作坊，参与流程的每个子单元各一次，其中三次工作坊包含支持性单元
> - 每次工作坊安排一整天（共 13 天）；工作坊由业务分析师主持，物流系统架构师提供支持（每次工作坊投入 16 小时工作量）；每个工作坊日的产出是会上达成一致的 BPMN 流程模型，以及一份流程说明工作表，其中包含 IT 系统在各流程步骤中的作用
> - 安排两（2）天用于工作坊排期、模板及其他材料准备
> - 安排两（2）天供业务分析师汇总全部材料，三（3）天供牵头架构师评审
>
>   这些活动的产出是细化后的流程模型与说明。
>
> - 安排一（1）周用于子单元评审，评审并行进行（评审期间项目团队为支持子单元投入 16 小时工作量）
> - 安排四（4）天用于处理问题
> - 安排一（1）天召开子单元工作坊，讨论问题处理情况，并沟通下一阶段（流程建模）的计划
>
> 该阶段计划记录在一份专门的工作表中，列出活动、执行这些活动的资源、时间框架与工作产品。之后又补充了工作坊细节——日期、地点与子单元代表。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 阶段详细规划在开始前完成，利用前一阶段实际结果、风险和经验。
  - 适用边界：总体项目计划与阶段详细计划粒度不同，不强制启动时穷尽所有未来细节。
  - PDF 62：before it starts
- 细化阶段计划仍须服从已设定的工作量和进度边界。
  - 适用边界：若超界应走变更/例外流程，不得把重排当作无须批准的修复。
  - PDF 63：within the stage boundaries

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0062:c006 · PDF 62**

3.3.2. Planning a Stage

**G188:p0062:c007 · PDF 62**

According to PRINCE2, a stage should be planned before it starts; i.e., at the end of the previous stage. This enables use of earlier project results, risks, and lessons learned in stage planning. It also allows for task execution and monitoring from the beginning. Typically, stage boundaries are already set in the Project Plan (refer to Section 3.1), so when planning a stage, we already know:

**G188:p0062:c008 · PDF 62**

• What products are to be delivered by the stage; i.e., what views

**G188:p0062:c009 · PDF 62**

• What level of detail is expected

**G188:p0062:c010 · PDF 62**

• What architecture elements are in scope; e.g., processes and/or applications list for segment architecture

**G188:p0062:c011 · PDF 62**

• What the timeframe is

**G188:p0062:c012 · PDF 62**

• What the key tasks to be completed are

**G188:p0062:c013 · PDF 62**

• What resources are allocated

**G188:p0062:c014 · PDF 62**

If the above are unclear, use techniques described in Section 3.2 to determine them.

**G188:p0062:c015 · PDF 62**

Depending on the overall Project Plan level of detail, the stage plan can already be a part of it (i.e., the level of detail is sufficient to proceed with the work) or it may be needed to create a more detailed plan for the stage.

**G188:p0063:c000 · PDF 63**

PRINCE2 recommends planning the work stage by stage (see PRINCE2, §2.4). This approach is suitable in most Architecture Project cases, in our view. It is practical to have a general Project Plan that clearly defines stages and their boundaries, but does not go too deep — it should only outline the major activities and their timeframes within each stage. Therefore, a detailed stage plan has to be prepared before each of the stages.

**G188:p0063:c001 · PDF 63**

To create a stage plan, you can follow the guidelines on the Project Plan presented in Section 3.2. The approach is the same — defining activities, sequencing them, estimating efforts, and scheduling. However, effort and schedule should stay within the stage boundaries set by the Project Plan.

**G188:p0063:c002 · PDF 63**

Example 3-28

**G188:p0063:c003 · PDF 63**

In Project B, after the Process Overview stage, the Project Manager prepared the next stage (Process Analysis) plan.

**G188:p0063:c004 · PDF 63**

The Project Manager’s work was based on an earlier prepared Project Plan, which outlined the stage schedule:

**G188:p0063:c005 · PDF 63**

• Three (3) weeks for workshops with the subunits

**G188:p0063:c006 · PDF 63**

• One (1) week for analysis consolidation and internal review

**G188:p0063:c007 · PDF 63**

• One (1) week for analysis review by the subunits

**G188:p0063:c008 · PDF 63**

• One (1) week for addressing issues identified through reviews

**G188:p0063:c009 · PDF 63**

Additional information was collected in the previous stage (Process Overview), including:

**G188:p0063:c010 · PDF 63**

• There are 13 subunits that take an important part in the logistics process execution; Three others have a supporting role

**G188:p0063:c011 · PDF 63**

• Contact persons were established for each of the subunits

**G188:p0063:c012 · PDF 63**

Based on this input, the Project Manager detailed the stage plan with a daily granularity.

**G188:p0063:c013 · PDF 63**

• There will be 13 workshops, one for each of the subunits that take part in the process, with supporting units included in three of the workshops

**G188:p0063:c014 · PDF 63**

• One full day was allocated for each of the workshops (13 days overall); workshops are led by a business analyst with a logistics systems architect support (16 hours effort per workshop); the product of each workshop day is a BPMN process model agreed at the meeting and a worksheet with a process description including the IT systems role in the process steps

**G188:p0063:c015 · PDF 63**

• Two (2) days were allocated for workshops scheduling, templates, and other materials preparation

**G188:p0063:c016 · PDF 63**

• Two (2) days were allocated for business analyst to consolidate the whole material and Three (3) days for the leading architect to review

**G188:p0063:c017 · PDF 63**

The product of these activities are refined process model and descriptions

**G188:p0064:c000 · PDF 64**

• One (1) week was allocated for subunit reviews that would be conducted in parallel (16 hours effort of the Project Team for subunits support during the review period)

**G188:p0064:c001 · PDF 64**

• Four (4) days were allocated for addressing issues

**G188:p0064:c002 · PDF 64**

• One (1) day was allocated for a workshop with subunits to discuss how the issues were addressed and communicate plans for the next stage (Process Modeling)

**G188:p0064:c003 · PDF 64**

This stage plan was documented in a dedicated worksheet, listing the activities, resources performing these activities, timeframes, and work products. It was later supplemented with workshop details – dates, locations, and subunit representatives.

</details>

<a id="3.3.3"></a>
## 调整项目计划

原文：3.3.3. Adjusting a Project Plan。PDF 物理页：[64]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

理想情况下，规划阶段之后项目计划无需变更，即阶段计划与项目计划所隐含的进度、范围与预算限制相匹配。但如有需要，应更新项目计划以反映阶段计划。

项目仍须满足其时间/预算/范围边界——否则该变更须按例外处理。关于如何管理此类例外，参见 3.4 节。

阶段规划活动在 PRINCE2 中属于“管理阶段边界（Managing Stage Boundaries）”流程的一部分。PMBOK 建议在分阶段方式下，于每个阶段开始时执行其启动（Initiating）流程。更多信息参见第 4 章与第 5 章的映射以及项目管理方法本身。

> **示例 3-29**
>
> 在项目 B 中，流程概览阶段之后发现还有另一个业务单元参与物流流程执行。项目经理评估认为，流程分析阶段需要额外一周用于附加工作坊。项目经理还指出，流程建模阶段的进度非常紧张，额外的评审可能导致无法在四周内结束该阶段。
>
> 项目 B 由架构委员会治理，其发起人是 CIO。完成流程概览阶段后，项目经理向发起人与架构委员会报告阶段结束，呈报阶段成果，并请求变更项目进度（增加两（2）周）与预算（增加 120 小时），以反映所需的额外工作量。
>
> 经讨论，治理机构同意在流程分析阶段增加一（1）周（及 60 小时）。流程建模阶段时间不足的风险被登记，将在流程分析阶段之后重新评估。此外，各方同意每个子单元在流程建模阶段的评审中只派一名代表，以降低上述风险。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 项目计划可以更新，但超出时间、预算或范围边界的变更按例外处理。
  - 适用边界：新预测不是自动改写已批准基线；应保留申请、批准结果及剩余风险。
  - PDF 64：handled as an exception
- 案例3-29申请两周/120小时，批准仅一周/60小时并保留剩余风险。
  - 适用边界：申请值、授权值和风险应分别保存；不能把申请视为批准。
  - PDF 64：agreed for an extra one week

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0064:c004 · PDF 64**

3.3.3. Adjusting a Project Plan

**G188:p0064:c005 · PDF 64**

Ideally, there would be no changes to the Project Plan after planning a stage — i.e., the stage plan would match the schedule, scope, and budget restrictions implied by the Project Plan. However, if needed, the Project Plan should be updated to reflect the stage plan.

**G188:p0064:c006 · PDF 64**

The project still has to meet its time/budget/scope boundaries — or the change has to be handled as an exception. Please refer to Section 3.4 for how to manage such exceptions.

**G188:p0064:c007 · PDF 64**

Example 3-29

**G188:p0064:c008 · PDF 64**

In Project B, after the Process Overview stage it was identified that there is another business unit engaged in the logistics process execution. The Project Manager assessed that an extra week will be needed for additional workshops in the Process Analysis stage. The Project Manager also noted that the Process Modeling stage schedule was very tight and additional reviews could make it impossible to close the stage in four weeks.

**G188:p0064:c009 · PDF 64**

Project B was governed by an Architecture Board. Its Sponsor was the CIO. After completing the Process Overview stage, the Project Manager reported the stage end to the Sponsor and the Architecture Board, presented its results, and requested a change in the project schedule (plus two (2) weeks) and budget (plus 120 hours) to reflect the extra effort needed.

**G188:p0064:c010 · PDF 64**

After discussion, the governing body agreed for an extra one week (and 60 hours) in the Process Analysis stage. A risk of insufficient time to complete the Process Modeling stage was registered. It would be reassessed after the Process Analysis stage. Also, it was agreed that each of the subunits would select a single representative for reviews in the Process Modeling stage to reduce the above risk.

**G188:p0064:c011 · PDF 64**

Stage planning activities are described as part of the Managing Stage Boundaries process in PRINCE2. PMBOK recommends performing its Initiating processes at the start of each stage when using a staged approach. Refer to the mappings presented in Chapter 4 and Chapter 5 and the Project Management methods themselves for further information.

</details>

<a id="3.4"></a>
## 执行、监控与控制阶段

原文：3.4. Execute, Monitor, and Control a Stage。PDF 物理页：[65]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

> PRINCE2 与 PMBOK 都区分对阶段的监控与控制同对工作的指导或执行。这两组流程目的不同、关注的具体流程不同；但在日常项目管理惯例中，它们并行执行。出于实用考虑，本文档把两组内容——指导（PRINCE2）或执行（PMBOK）以及监控与控制——合为一章呈现。

> 更多细节请参阅 PRINCE2 与 PMBOK 方法。

执行、监控与控制阶段是项目经理在项目每个阶段的核心活动。这意味着确保工作被妥善分派、跟踪工作进展、对未曾预见的情况作出反应，并确保把恰当的信息分发给需要的相关方。

PRINCE2 方法为该流程指派的活动不多。从 PMBOK 角度看，这些活动属于监控与控制（Monitor & Control），只是缩小到项目的特定阶段。由于多数行动在整个阶段持续进行，难以设定次序或描述先后。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 执行与监控控制目的不同但在日常工作中并行。
  - 适用边界：本章排序不是每次必须串行跑完的一次性流程。
  - PDF 65：executed in parallel

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0065:c000 · PDF 65**

3.4. Execute, Monitor, and Control a Stage

**G188:p0065:c001 · PDF 65**

Both PRINCE2 and PMBOK distinguish between monitoring and controlling the stage and directing or executing the work. These groups of processes serve a different purpose and focus on different individual processes; however, from daily Project Management routines they are executed in parallel. For practical reasons in this document we decided to present both groups — directing (PRINCE2) or executing (PMBOK) and monitoring and control — as a one chapter covering both areas.

**G188:p0065:c002 · PDF 65**

For more details, please refer to the PRINCE2 and PMBOK methods.

**G188:p0065:c003 · PDF 65**

Execution, monitoring, and controlling a stage is a core activity of the Project Manager within every stage of the project. This means making sure that the work is being properly assigned, tracking work progress, and reacting to unforeseen situations, as well as making sure that the proper information is being distributed to the required parties.

**G188:p0065:c004 · PDF 65**

There are few activities assigned to this process by the PRINCE2 method. From the PMBOK perspective, these activities are a part of Monitor & Control, just narrowed down to the specific phase of the project. Since most of the actions are ongoing throughout the stage it is difficult to set the sequence or describe the order.

</details>

<a id="3.4.1"></a>
## 授权项目工作包

原文：3.4.1. Authorize the Project Work Package。PDF 物理页：[65]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

按关键规则，未经项目经理明确授权，不得开展任何项目工作包的工作。其目的是防止在所有前提条件完成并经过适当审批之前就着手某些交付物或任务，从而使项目偏离正轨；否则，返工或交付不符合质量要求产品的风险很高。

开始工作的授权应由项目经理明确给出，并附以下信息：

- 项目工作包应交付什么、范围与预期产品；
- 交付产品工作的成本与时间及其容差——容差常来自进度的自由浮动时间或其他约束，需由项目经理识别并沟通；
- 本流程所需的质量要求与配置管理的具体信息；
- 所需报告与信息分发的具体信息，以支持对进展的控制与监控。

还应向团队明确沟通：在开始任何与项目工作包相关的活动之前，必须获得授权。即便团队认为所有前提条件均已完成，仍可能存在（且常常存在）需要先解决的其他问题，之后工作才能继续、与产品相关的所有细节才能被恰当界定。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 项目工作包开始前需项目经理明确授权，确保前提及审批完成。
  - 适用边界：成员认为已准备好不等于获得授权；智能能力生成任务也不自动授权团队执行。
  - PDF 65：unless clear authorization is given
- 授权须传递范围/产品、成本时间容差、质量配置要求及报告安排。
  - 适用边界：不能只给任务名称和截止日期即称为完整工作包授权。
  - PDF 65：cost and time

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0065:c005 · PDF 65**

3.4.1. Authorize the Project Work Package

**G188:p0065:c006 · PDF 65**

According to the key rule, no Project Work Package should be worked on unless clear authorization is given by the Project Manager. The purpose is to prevent the project going off track by working on some deliverables or tasks until all prerequisites are completed and properly processed through approval. Otherwise, the risk of rework or delivery of a product that does not comply with quality requirements is high.

**G188:p0065:c007 · PDF 65**

The authorization to start the work should be explicitly received from the Project Manager along with the following information:

**G188:p0065:c008 · PDF 65**

• What should be delivered, the scope, and expected product of the Project Work Package

**G188:p0065:c009 · PDF 65**

• The cost and time of work on the delivery of the product and its tolerances that often come from the free-float of the schedule or other constraints that need to be identified and communicated by the Project Manager

**G188:p0065:c010 · PDF 65**

• Specific information about quality requirements and configuration management required for this process

**G188:p0065:c011 · PDF 65**

• Specific information about required reporting and distribution of information that would support the control and monitoring of progress

**G188:p0065:c012 · PDF 65**

It should also by clearly communicated to the team that authorization is required before they can start any activity related to the Project Work Package. Even if the team feels like all prerequisites are completed, there might be – and often are – situations where there are additional questions that need to be addressed before the work can continue and all details related to the product are properly defined.

</details>

<a id="3.4.2"></a>
## 审查项目工作包状态

原文：3.4.2. Review Project Work Package Status。PDF 物理页：[66]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

按项目计划与项目工作包授权步骤的界定，承担工作的团队应按约定的固定间隔向项目经理提交报告（检查点报告，checkpoint reports），以便持续控制进展，并识别与项目工作包按时完成相关的风险。

团队提供的输入是项目经理向利益相关者报告进展、就进度、预算及其他相关预测作出必要调整决策的基础。这也是识别可能影响既定截止日期、质量或成本基线的风险与问题的关键环节。

实践中，项目工作包状态审查以状态会议形式进行，参加者为项目管理团队与负责该工作包的项目工作包团队。强烈建议在书面形式之外再以会议形式进行状态审查，因为这样可以获得更多信息与细节，并讨论任何不清楚之处。

审查项目工作包状态还可识别项目或产品中工作未按原计划推进的领域。这可能由多种内部与外部因素造成，例如低估完成工作所需的时间与工作量，或项目范围、进度、工作量（预算）发生其他变化。任何变更都应遵循正式变更控制程序。

变更控制程序从项目管理角度看极为重要：它确保作为项目产品、进度或成本基线而商定交付的内容处于受控状态，只能以正式方式变更。该程序因项目与组织而异：有时项目经理拥有一定权限就项目变更作出决定（例如不超过某一成本阈值），有时只有项目指导委员会才能批准所请求的变更。非常重要的一点是：变更在获批之前不得纳入项目。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 团队按约定间隔报告检查点状态，为项目预测、风险及变更决策提供依据。
  - 适用边界：报告频率按项目约定，不能将周报示例设为通用规范。
  - PDF 66：regular, agreed intervals
- 变更须经有权限者批准才纳入项目。
  - 适用边界：审批权可能被委派给项目经理，也可能保留在委员会；不可总是自动升级或自动批准。
  - PDF 66：until it is approved

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0066:c000 · PDF 66**

3.4.2. Review Project Work Package Status

**G188:p0066:c001 · PDF 66**

As defined in the Project Plan and Project Work Package authorization step, the team that is carrying out the work should submit the reports (checkpoint reports) to the Project Manager at regular, agreed intervals to allow for continuous control of progress and to identify risks related to timely completion of the Project Work Package.

**G188:p0066:c002 · PDF 66**

The input received from teams is the base for the Project Manager to report the progress to stakeholders and make decisions for required adjustments for schedule, budget, and other relevant forecasts. This is also a key point for identification of risks and problems that may impact assumed deadlines, quality, or cost baseline.

**G188:p0066:c003 · PDF 66**

In practice, the review of the Project Work Package status is carried out in a form of status meetings with the Project Management team and Project Work Package team working on the particular package. It is highly recommended to perform the status review in meeting form in addition to written form, since more information and details can be obtained and any unclear points can be discussed.

**G188:p0066:c004 · PDF 66**

Reviewing the status of the Project Work Packages also allows identification of the areas of the project or products where work is not being progressed as originally planned. This may happen due to multiple internal and external factors. It might be a case of underestimation of the time and effort needed to complete the work, or any other change in the Project Scope, schedule, or effort (budget). Any change should be subject to the formal change control procedure.

**G188:p0066:c005 · PDF 66**

The change control procedure is extremely important from a Project Management perspective. It is to ensure that whatever was agreed to be delivered as project products, schedule, or cost baseline is under control, and can be changed only in a formal way. The procedure varies between projects and organizations: sometimes the Project Manager has certain authority to make decisions for changes in the project (for example, not exceeding a certain cost threshold), and in some cases only the Steering Committee of the project can approve the change requested. It is very important that the change is not incorporated in the project until it is approved.

</details>

<a id="3.4.3"></a>
## 审查已完成的项目工作包

原文：3.4.3. Review Completed Project Work Package。PDF 物理页：[66]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

一旦项目工作包的工作完成、该工作包的最终产品就绪并获批，项目经理即把该工作包标记为完成，相关的后续项目工作包便可按“授权项目工作包”任务所述获得授权。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 项目工作包只有最终产品就绪且获批后才标记完成。
  - 适用边界：后续包可再获授权，不是前包完成后自动开始。
  - PDF 66：ready and approved

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0066:c006 · PDF 66**

3.4.3. Review Completed Project Work Package

**G188:p0066:c007 · PDF 66**

Once the work on the Project Work Package is completed and the final product of the package is ready and approved, the Project Work Package is marked complete by the Project Manager, and related successor Project Work Packages can be authorized as described in the Authorizing Project Work Package task.

</details>

<a id="3.4.4"></a>
## 审查阶段状态

原文：3.4.4. Review Stage Status。PDF 物理页：[66]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

项目经理必须审查阶段的总体状态，以确保对进展的控制。为有效做到这一点，项目经理应审查所有管理产品，如检查点报告、当前阶段计划、各类登记册与日志（质量登记册、问题登记册、风险登记册与每日日志），以及收益评审计划。基于这些输入，估计并记录项目总体状态（例如使用 MS Project® 工具），以向利益相关者呈现阶段或项目的总体状态。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 阶段状态汇总需合并进度、质量、问题、风险及收益评审计划。
  - 适用边界：某个包完成不代表全阶段、项目或收益目标完成。
  - PDF 66：including the benefits review plan

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0066:c008 · PDF 66**

3.4.4. Review Stage Status

**G188:p0066:c009 · PDF 66**

The Project Manager must review the overall status of the stage in order to ensure control over the progress being made. To effectively do that, the Project Manager should review all management products such as checkpoint reports, the current stage plan, the various registers and logs (quality register, issue register, risk register, and daily log), also including the benefits review plan. Based on

**G188:p0066:c010 · PDF 66**

®

**G188:p0066:c011 · PDF 66**

these inputs, the overall project status is estimated and recorded (for example, using the MS Project tool). This should present the overall stage or project status to stakeholders.

</details>

<a id="3.4.5"></a>
## 捕获并审查问题与风险

原文：3.4.5. Capture and Examine Issues and Risks。PDF 物理页：[67, 68]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

在项目执行与控制阶段，风险管理变得更为关键。项目经理应定期进行风险评估，尤其应注意生产切换（production cutover）之前的风险。

按照项目启动期间确立的策略、流程与程序，项目经理必须确保所有项目问题都被仔细、准确地记录、排定优先级并指派关闭责任人。此外，项目经理有责任推动未决问题关闭，且关闭须满足要求。

项目经理应定期进行风险评估，应执行以下步骤：

- 重新审视风险日志中已定义的风险，并酌情纳入新风险；
- 对每个风险进行分类、评估并分析其概率与严重程度；
- 确定可表明风险可能正在发生的征兆；
- 为每个风险编制缓解与应对计划；
- 报告风险评估结果。

> **示例 3-30**
>
> 项目经理决定每周召开状态会议，以跟踪阶段进展并控制过程。由于项目时间跨度达数月，他决定每周一次会议已足够。若进度更激进，更频繁的会议可能更好，以便加快信息流动、缩短对任何计划外情况的反应时间。每周会议并不取代日常沟通与信息交换，但它是确保向团队统一传达信息并收集反馈的最佳工具。
>
> 项目经理围绕一个通用框架为会议准备议程：
>
> - **回顾迄今进展**——这使项目经理能够收集并讨论阶段状态信息、项目工作包完成情况的某些细节，并识别与讨论可能影响既定工作计划的风险。
>
>   同时，确认已完成的项目工作包，项目经理确保项目工作包的最终版本已提交审批流程。
>
> - **确认下一时期（通常为一周）的计划**——这属于状态控制的一部分，即确认下周要开展的任务。
>
>   对于延续当前项目工作包的任务，这提供了该时期预期取得何种进展的信息。若项目计划规定下一时期启动新的项目工作包，则需向团队明确传达；说明是否已“放行”开始工作，或在放行前还需要完成什么。
>
> - **识别并审查风险与问题**——多数风险与问题来自团队成员。他们能识别多数可能影响项目的潜在情况（输入数据缺失、额外工作、误解与期望管理不当等）；项目经理也基于其信息来源分享来自项目周边环境的信息，作为潜在风险或问题加以讨论。风险或问题（问题是已实际发生的风险）一经识别，即由项目经理记录在简单电子表格中，以便后续跟踪。
>
> - **项目经理决定下一步需要做什么**——通常他会与受该风险或问题影响的所有相关方召开简短会议，进一步审查并规划缓解措施。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 问题须准确记录、排序并分配关闭责任，推动满足要求的关闭。
  - 适用边界：标记已记录不能代替解决；风险发生征兆和应对计划也应持续复核。
  - PDF 67：assigned for closure
- 案例将问题描述为已实际发生的风险。
  - 适用边界：保留本指南案例措辞，不把它强制为所有问题都必须先有风险记录的本体约束。
  - PDF 68：issue is a materialized risk

**G188:p0067:c000 · PDF 67**

3.4.5. Capture and Examine Issues and Risks

**G188:p0067:c001 · PDF 67**

Risk management becomes even more critical during the project execution and control phase. It is important for the Project Manager to regularly conduct risk assessments. Particular attention should be paid to risks prior to production cutover.

**G188:p0067:c002 · PDF 67**

Following the strategy, processes, and procedures established during project start-up, the Project Manager must make sure that all project issues are carefully and accurately logged, prioritized, and assigned for closure. Moreover, it is the Project Manager’s responsibility to drive open issues to a closure that fulfills the requirements.

**G188:p0067:c003 · PDF 67**

On a regular basis, the Project Manager should perform risk assessments. The following steps should be performed:

**G188:p0067:c004 · PDF 67**

• Revisit risks defined in the risk log and include new risks as appropriate

**G188:p0067:c005 · PDF 67**

• Categorize, assess, and analyze the probability and severity of each risk

**G188:p0067:c006 · PDF 67**

• Determine symptoms that would indicate that risks may be occurring

**G188:p0067:c007 · PDF 67**

• Prepare a mitigation and response plan for each risk

**G188:p0067:c008 · PDF 67**

• Report results of risk assessment

**G188:p0067:c009 · PDF 67**

Example 3-30

**G188:p0067:c010 · PDF 67**

The Project Manager decides to hold a weekly status meeting to keep track of the stage progress and control the process. Since the project timeline extends over a few months, he decides that a weekly meeting is sufficient. In case of more aggressive schedules more frequent meetings may be better to allow for faster information flow and shorter reaction time to any unplanned situations. A weekly meeting does not replace a daily routine for communication and information exchange, but is the best tool to ensure unified communication to the team and collecting feedback.

**G188:p0067:c011 · PDF 67**

The Project Manager prepares the agenda for the meetings around a common schema:

**G188:p0067:c012 · PDF 67**

• Review of progress to date — this allows the Project Manager to collect and discuss information about the status of the stage, some details on Project Work Packages completion, and to identify and discuss risks that may impact the assumed work plan

**G188:p0067:c013 · PDF 67**

At the same time, completed Project Work Packages are confirmed and the Project Manager makes sure that the final versions of the Project Work Packages are delivered for the approval process.

**G188:p0067:c014 · PDF 67**

• Confirm the plan for next period of time (usually a week) — this is part of the status control in terms of confirmation of tasks to be worked on during the following week.

**G188:p0067:c015 · PDF 67**

In case of tasks that are a continuation of current Project Work Packages, this serves as information about what progress is expected to be achieved over the period. If the Project

**G188:p0068:c000 · PDF 68**

Plan defines a new Project Work Package to start in the next period, this needs to be clearly communicated to the team; if there is a “green light” to start the work, or what needs to happen before the green light is given.

**G188:p0068:c001 · PDF 68**

• Identify and examine risks and issues – most risks and issues come from team members.

**G188:p0068:c002 · PDF 68**

They can identify most potential situations (missing input data, additional work, misunderstandings and mismanaged expectations, etc.) that may impact the project, but also information from project surroundings that is shared by the Project Manager based on his sources of information is discussed as a potential risk or issue. Once identified, risks or issues (issue is a materialized risk) are documented in a simple spreadsheet by the Project Manager for further tracking.

**G188:p0068:c003 · PDF 68**

• The Project Manager decides what needs to be done next – usually he holds a quick meeting with all parties that are affected by the risk or issue to examine it further and plan for mitigation

</details>

<a id="3.4.6"></a>
## 报告要点（Highlight Report）

原文：3.4.6. Report Highlights。PDF 物理页：[68, 69]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

基于收集的信息，项目经理应构建恰当且有针对性的信息，向项目利益相关者沟通。这涉及编制按时间驱动的要点报告（Highlight Report），呈现项目当前成果与实际进展，描述项目经理可能打算采取的纠正措施，以及对阶段剩余部分与项目剩余的预测。

向利益相关者提供的详细程度因项目及具体利益相关者而异，应视具体情况并考虑每位利益相关者的实际要求与偏好而定。实践中，最低限度的细节应包括：

- 上一报告期的成果与已完成任务，说明做了什么及其与原项目进度和计划的关系；
- 下一时期计划的关键活动——重要的是，将上一报告期列出的计划任务，与本期对同一任务集合报告的实际成果对应起来，以保持计划与进展的连续可追踪性；
- 已识别的关键风险与问题，尤其是需要利益相关者关注或上报的；
- 报告往往还包含对已批准与未批准变更（通过正式变更控制流程引入）的跟踪、未决问题，或需利益相关者作出的决策。

> **示例 3-31**
>
> 基于每周状态会议收集的信息，项目经理按沟通计划的规定，为管理层与利益相关者编制状态报告。报告的形式与内容取决于报告对象的期望与偏好。有些人偏好简单的一页纸进展摘要，包含下一时期的关键活动、关键风险与问题；另一些利益相关者则期望详细的演示，深入讨论风险与已交付项目工作包的内容。
>
> 对本项目，项目经理与关键利益相关者讨论了期望：他需要编制一页纸的每周报告，突出关键活动与风险，并在每周简报会上用 15 分钟与利益相关者讨论。项目经理决定每个方面列出三条：迄今成果、下一时期计划、三到四项最重要的风险与问题，以及需由利益相关者作出的最重要决策。
>
> 每月编制一次更详细的报告，并在项目指导委员会的 45 分钟会议上呈报。报告呈现更多讨论与细节，包括下一时期（此处为一个月）与公司其他员工召开团队互动会议的安排，以与决策者确认进度、确保其知情与支持，以及关于风险与问题的更多细节，特别是在需要上报时。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 报告将本期实际成果与前期计划的同一任务集合对应。
  - 适用边界：不得仅滚动替换计划掩盖未完成事项；原计划、实际结果和新预测需分开。
  - PDF 69：same list of achievements
- 要点报告包括实际进展、拟采取纠正措施和剩余工作预测。
  - 适用边界：拟采取不是已实施，预测不是实际；详略按利益相关者需要裁剪。
  - PDF 68：actual progress

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0068:c004 · PDF 68**

3.4.6. Report Highlights

**G188:p0068:c005 · PDF 68**

Based on collected information, the Project Manager should build the proper and tailored message that should be communicated to the project stakeholders. This involves creating the time-driven Highlight Report, which presents the current achievements and actual progress of the project, and also describes any corrective actions that the Project Manager may intend to carry out, as well as forecasts for the remainder of the stage and the project.

**G188:p0068:c006 · PDF 68**

The level of detail that should be provided to stakeholders varies between projects and each particular stakeholder. It should be defined per particular situation taking into account the actual requirements and particular preferences of each and every stakeholder. In practice, the minimum set of details should be:

**G188:p0068:c007 · PDF 68**

• Achievements and tasks completed over the last reporting period to explain what was done and how it related to the original project schedule and plan

**G188:p0069:c000 · PDF 69**

• Key activities planned for the next period — it is important, and helps to keep track of progress made, to keep both points integral by showing the same list of achievements that was presented in the last period as the planned tasks

**G188:p0069:c001 · PDF 69**

• Key risks and problems identified, especially these that require the attention of the stakeholders or escalation

**G188:p0069:c002 · PDF 69**

• Quite often the report contains also some tracking for changes unapproved and approved that were introduced to the project (using the formal change control process), open issues, or decisions that need to be made by stakeholders

**G188:p0069:c003 · PDF 69**

Example 3-31

**G188:p0069:c004 · PDF 69**

Based on the information collected during the weekly status meeting, the Project Manager prepares the status report for management and stakeholders, as defined in the communication plan. The form and content of the report depends on the expectations and preferences of the report audience. Some prefer a simple one-page summary of progress to date, key activities for the next period of time, key risks, and issues; while other stakeholders expect detailed presentations with a deep-dive on risks and content of the delivered Project Work Packages.

**G188:p0069:c005 · PDF 69**

For this project, the Project Manager discussed the expectations with key stakeholders, and he is expected to prepare a one-page weekly report, highlighting the key activities and risks, and to discuss it with stakeholders on the weekly briefing within 15 minutes. The Project Manager decides to present three bullets per area: achievements to date, plans for the next period, three or four of the most important risks and issues, and also the most important decisions to be made by the stakeholders.

**G188:p0069:c006 · PDF 69**

Once per month a more detailed report is prepared and presented to the Steering Committee of the project during a 45-minute meeting. More discussion and details are presented, including a plan for team interaction meetings with other employees of the company for the next period of time (one month in this case), to confirm the schedule with decision-makers and ensure their awareness and support, and more details on risks and issues especially if escalation is required.

</details>

<a id="3.4.7"></a>
## 上报问题与风险

原文：3.4.7. Escalate Issues and Risks。PDF 物理页：[69, 70]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

当处理某项风险或问题超出项目经理权限时，须上报项目委员会/指导委员会；当风险或问题超出分配给它的容差时尤须如此。PRINCE2 把这定义为例外（exception），并触发例外报告（Exception Report）——由项目经理编写并提交指导委员会以采取进一步行动。

> **示例 3-32**
>
> 与关键利益相关者及指导委员会举行的 15 分钟状态会议，是把需在指挥链中处理的风险或问题上报的绝佳场合。当然，这些事项多数已在风险识别后由项目经理在会前与利益相关者讨论过；但这些会议提供了进一步讨论的机会，或许还能获得管理层团队或指导委员会中其他人的参与与支持。对于未被认定超出项目经理权限、但仍可能严重影响项目的风险与问题，项目经理决定同时分享其缓解计划以沟通此类情况；而且——往往如此——他会得到指导委员会的支持，使缓解过程更为顺利。
>
> 在项目 E 中，架构师发现某些数据仓库计算应使用银行已有的商用现货（COTS）计算引擎方案完成。然而，业务分析师团队对该方案一无所知。存在一种风险：他们可能无法充分按照计算引擎的能力对计算过程建模。因此，架构师与项目经理协作描述该风险，并记录在风险日志中。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 超过项目经理权限或容差的风险/问题应上报治理机构并触发例外报告。
  - 适用边界：严重但仍在权限内的事项也可沟通求助；不能等定期会议才披露已识别超界问题。
  - PDF 69：exceeds the authority

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0069:c007 · PDF 69**

3.4.7. Escalate Issues and Risks

**G188:p0069:c008 · PDF 69**

In cases where handling a risk or problem exceeds the authority of the Project Manager, it is necessary to escalate to the Project Board/Steering Committee. This must be done especially when a risk or problem exceeds the tolerance assigned to it. PRINCE2 defines this as an exception and triggers an Exception Report, which is written by the Project Manager and submitted to the Steering Committee for further action.

**G188:p0069:c009 · PDF 69**

Example 3-32

**G188:p0069:c010 · PDF 69**

The 15-minute status meetings with key stakeholders and the Steering Committee are the perfect occasion to escalate risks or issues that need to be taken up in the chain of command. Of course

**G188:p0070:c000 · PDF 70**

most of these issues are discussed by the Project Manager with stakeholders before the meeting, once risks are identified, but these meetings present a good opportunity to discuss them further and perhaps get the involvement and support of someone else from the management team or Steering Committee. For risks and issues that were not identified as exceeding the authority of the Project Manager, but may still impact the project heavily, the Project Manager decides to share his mitigation plan as well to communicate such a situation, but also — quite often — he is given support by Steering Committee board that makes the mitigation process easier.

**G188:p0070:c001 · PDF 70**

In Project E, the architect identifies that some of the data warehouse calculations should be done in a COTS calculation engine solution possessed by the bank. However, the business analyst team does not have any knowledge of this solution. There is a risk that they would be unable to model the calculations process adequately to the calculation engine capabilities. Therefore, the architect collaborates with the Project Manager to describe the risk. It is recorded in the risk log.

</details>

<a id="3.4.8"></a>
## 采取纠正措施

原文：3.4.8. Take Corrective Action。PDF 物理页：[70]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

在此阶段，项目工作包会对照现有计划进行修订，风险评估、问题以及计划与项目工作包之间的差异在此审查中完成。阶段状态审查常以如下结论结束：需要采取某些行动使项目重回正轨。这些就是纠正措施，应成为项目计划与进度的组成部分。目标是处理已经或预计会对计划与进度产生负面影响的所有情况。

> **示例 3-33**
>
> 基于对风险或问题的评估以及与指导委员会或利益相关者的讨论，制定一项计划，以确保风险或问题对项目的负面影响最小（或正面影响最大）。项目经理把这些任务加入受影响的项目工作包所对应的项目计划中。任务也被放入时间线，并像项目其他任务一样指派任务负责人。纠正措施成为项目的组成部分，并从此时起按计划中任何其他任务同样的方式被跟踪、报告与控制。
>
> 接上例，项目 E 的项目经理与架构师及业务分析负责人协作设计风险缓解措施。他们建议对两名业务分析师进行计算引擎方案能力培训；随后，他们将运用这些知识指导计算过程分析，以充分利用计算引擎的能力。
>
> 之后，项目经理与管理层团队协作，正式接受该缓解措施并予以推进。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 纠正措施纳入计划、时间线和责任分配，随后像其他任务一样跟踪。
  - 适用边界：仅描述风险缓解建议不是完成缓解；案例还需管理层正式接受。
  - PDF 70：task owner is assigned

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0070:c002 · PDF 70**

3.4.8. Take Corrective Action

**G188:p0070:c003 · PDF 70**

During this stage, the Project Work Package is revised in comparison with the existing plan. Evaluation of risks, problems, and discrepancies between the plan and the Project Work Package are done as part of this review. The stage status often ends with a conclusion that certain actions need to be taken to bring the project back on track. These are corrective actions and they should become the integral part of the Project Plan and schedule. The goal is to address all situations that already did or are expected to impact the plan and schedule in negative form.

**G188:p0070:c004 · PDF 70**

Example 3-33

**G188:p0070:c005 · PDF 70**

Based on risk or issue evaluation and discussion with the Steering Committee or stakeholders, a plan is created to make sure the risk or issue has a minimal negative (or maximum positive) impact on the project. The Project Manager adds these tasks to the Project Plan in the Project Work Package that is affected by the risk or issue. Tasks are also put on the timeline and the task owner is assigned as for every other task of the project. Corrective action becomes an integral part of the project and from here on is being tracked, reported, and controlled in the same way as any other task in the plan.

**G188:p0070:c006 · PDF 70**

In reference to the previous example, the Project E Project Manager collaborates with the architect and the business analysis leader to design a risk mitigation. They recommend that two of the business analysts would be trained on the calculation engine solution capabilities. Then, they will use this knowledge to guide the calculations process analysis to make use of the calculation engine capabilities.

**G188:p0070:c007 · PDF 70**

Later on, the Project Manager collaborates with the management team to formally accept this mitigation and proceed with it.

</details>

<a id="3.5"></a>
## 阶段收尾

原文：3.5. Ending a Stage。PDF 物理页：[71]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

当阶段交付物完成并通常完成移交与批准时，阶段即结束。按 PMBOK，阶段末是一个自然的里程碑，可借此重新评估正在进行的工作并作出必要变更，甚至在需要时终止项目 [PMBOK, §2.4.2]。PRINCE2 对此更为具体——它定义了“管理阶段边界（Managing a Stage Boundary）”流程，其目的是审查已完成的阶段并批准下一阶段计划，确保项目仍然合理且可行 [PRINCE2, §17]。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 阶段末重新评估项目是否仍合理可行，并可变更或终止。
  - 适用边界：阶段结束不是默认批准下一阶段。
  - PDF 71：still justifiable and viable

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0071:c000 · PDF 71**

3.5. Ending a Stage

**G188:p0071:c001 · PDF 71**

A stage ends when stage deliverables are done and, typically, handed off and approved. According to PMBOK, stage end is a natural milestone that allows reassessment of the work being done and necessary changes to be made, or even termination of the project if needed [PMBOK, §2.4.2]. PRINCE2 is much more specific on the topic — it defines the Managing a Stage Boundary process whose purpose is to review the finished stage and approve next stage plan, making sure the project is still justifiable and viable [PRINCE2, §17].

</details>

<a id="3.5.1"></a>
## 阶段交付物移交与批准

原文：3.5.1. Stage Deliverables Hand-off and Approval。PDF 物理页：[71]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

在阶段末，项目经理确保所有阶段交付物都得到妥善移交与批准。这可能包括项目团队内部评审（例如将把交付物作为后续项目执行输入的人员）、外部方评审（如业务用户），以及利益相关者批准（如项目发起人或指导委员会成员）。

若每项交付物此前已分别获批，阶段移交与批准活动可能非常有限。

> **示例 3-34**
>
> 在项目 B 中，完成工作坊与流程分析交付物（流程模型与说明）的内部评审后，项目经理把这些交付物送交业务单元评审，设定一周的期限征求意见与变更建议。此后，项目团队评估并处理收到的评审意见，召开会议讨论主要问题。然后，交付物被呈报项目发起人作最终批准（见下一示例）。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 若交付物此前已逐项批准，阶段移交活动可缩减。
  - 适用边界：缩减仪式不删除每个交付物的批准与移交证据。
  - PDF 71：separately approved earlier

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0071:c002 · PDF 71**

3.5.1. Stage Deliverables Hand-off and Approval

**G188:p0071:c003 · PDF 71**

At the stage end, the Project Manager makes sure all of the stage deliverables are handed off and approved appropriately. This may include internal Project Team reviews (for example, those who will use the deliverables as inputs in further project execution), external parties’ reviews (such as business users), and stakeholders’ approval (for example, Project Sponsor or Steering Committee members).

**G188:p0071:c004 · PDF 71**

Stage hand-off and approval activities may be very limited, if each of the deliverables was separately approved earlier.

**G188:p0071:c005 · PDF 71**

Example 3-34

**G188:p0071:c006 · PDF 71**

In Project B, after completing workshops and internal reviews of the Process Analysis deliverables (process models and descriptions), the Project Manager sent these deliverables to business units for review. A one-week deadline was set for comments and change suggestions. After that, the Project Team assessed and addressed the reviews received. A meeting was held to discuss major issues. Then, the deliverables were presented to the Project Sponsor for final approval (see next example).

</details>

<a id="3.5.2"></a>
## 管理阶段边界

原文：3.5.2. Managing a Stage Boundary。PDF 物理页：[71, 72]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

当阶段退出准则满足时，项目经理应向架构项目监督机构（Architecture Project Oversight，即架构委员会或项目发起人；见 3.1 节）报告阶段结束。报告通常应包括：

- **已完成阶段的总结**——应说明所有阶段产品如何交付，以及阶段在时间、成本、质量、范围、风险与收益目标方面的绩效；
- **对项目的影响**——如项目计划是否需要更新；
- **下一阶段的计划**——阶段规划参见 3.2 节。

> **示例 3-35**
>
> 在项目 B 中，完成与业务单元的评审并处理其意见后，交付物被呈报项目发起人作最终批准。该会议同时也旨在结束流程分析阶段。因此，项目经理报告该阶段按时交付，呈报流程建模阶段计划并建议执行；该建议获得项目发起人批准。

所识别的任何问题应按 3.4.7 节所述处理。

架构项目监督机构决定项目的后续执行——继续、变更或终止。它可考虑项目内部因素（如及时性与成本）与外部因素（如企业目标与优先级的变动）。

> **示例 3-36**
>
> 在项目 E 中，高层架构阶段之后，交付物被呈报项目发起人作最终批准。但项目经理注意到，详细业务分析工作流识别出的缺口需要大量额外工作，且可能影响高层架构。他呈报了相关的项目风险，并建议调整项目计划，其中包括在两个月后详细业务分析全部完成后重新审视高层架构阶段。指导委员会接受了所提解决方案。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 监督机构根据内外因素决定继续、变更或终止项目。
  - 适用边界：架构师可以建议，决定属于有权治理角色。
  - PDF 72：continuation, change, or termination

**G188:p0071:c007 · PDF 71**

3.5.2. Managing a Stage Boundary

**G188:p0071:c008 · PDF 71**

When stage exit criteria are met, the stage end should be reported by the Project Manager to the Architecture Project Oversight (i.e., Architecture Board or Project Sponsor; see Section 3.1). Typically, the report should include:

**G188:p0071:c009 · PDF 71**

• A summary of the completed stage — it should show how all stage products were delivered and how the stage has performed against targets for time, cost, quality, scope, risk, and benefit

**G188:p0071:c010 · PDF 71**

• Impact on the project — if the Project Plan needs to be updated

**G188:p0071:c011 · PDF 71**

• Plan of the next stage — refer to Section 3.2 for stage planning

**G188:p0071:c012 · PDF 71**

Example 3-35

**G188:p0071:c013 · PDF 71**

In Project B, after completing reviews with the business units and addressing their comments, the deliverables were presented to the Project Sponsor for final approval. The meeting was also

**G188:p0072:c000 · PDF 72**

meant to close the Process Analysis stage. Therefore, the Project Manager reported on-time delivery of the stage, presented the Process Modeling stage plan, and recommended its execution, which was approved by the Project Sponsor.

**G188:p0072:c001 · PDF 72**

Any issues identified should be handled according to the description in Section 3.4.7.

**G188:p0072:c002 · PDF 72**

The Architecture Project Oversight decides on further execution of the project — its continuation, change, or termination. It can take into account factors that are internal (such as timeliness and costs) and external (such as enterprise goals and priorities change) to the project.

**G188:p0072:c003 · PDF 72**

Example 3-36

**G188:p0072:c004 · PDF 72**

In Project E, after the High-Level Architecture stage, the deliverables were presented to the Project Sponsor for final approval. However, the Project Manager noted that the detailed business analysis stream identified gaps that needed a lot of extra work and may impact the High-Level Architecture. He presented associated risks for the project and recommended an adjusted Project Plan that included revisiting the High-Level Architecture stage after the detailed business analysis is fully completed two months later. The Steering Committee accepted the proposed resolution.

</details>

<a id="3.6"></a>
## 架构项目收尾

原文：3.6. Architecture Project Closing。PDF 物理页：[72]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

架构项目收尾包括正式关闭项目的全部活动。当所有迭代或阶段完成、所有项目产品或项目工作包完成并经执行发起人批准后，项目经理开始收尾。若项目商业论证不再成立且项目治理决定关闭项目，也可启动收尾。

架构项目收尾包括以下活动：

- 产品移交；
- 准备并建议收尾；
- 评估项目并记录经验教训。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 正常完成并获批或商业论证失效且治理决定终止，均可启动收尾。
  - 适用边界：提前终止不是成功完成，需保留终止理由及已交付状态。
  - PDF 72：business case is no longer valid

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0072:c005 · PDF 72**

3.6. Architecture Project Closing

**G188:p0072:c006 · PDF 72**

Architecture Project closure includes all activities that formally close the project. The Project Manager starts closing the project when all iterations or stages are finished and all project products or Project Work Packages are finished and approved by the Executive. It can also be started if the project’s business case is no longer valid and project governance decides to close the project.

**G188:p0072:c007 · PDF 72**

Architecture Project closing includes the following activities:

**G188:p0072:c008 · PDF 72**

• Products handover

**G188:p0072:c009 · PDF 72**

• Prepare and recommend closure

**G188:p0072:c010 · PDF 72**

• Evaluate the project and document lessons learned

</details>

<a id="3.6.1"></a>
## 产品移交

原文：3.6.1. Products Handover。PDF 物理页：[72, 73, 74]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

架构项目交付待实施架构的定义。通向该架构实施的最后一步，是把交付物移交给负责实施的机构。

在阶段 F 结束时，架构项目交付若干共同构成最终产品的交付物。这些交付物——包括架构定义文档、实施与迁移计划以及架构路线图——应作为项目群与项目管理活动的输入，这些活动通常由另一组织单位执行（并受 ADM 阶段 G 治理）。

项目交付物应移交给将实施架构的组织单位（如 PMO 或实施工作的业务负责人）。根据我们的经验，确保这些交付物足以立即启动实施项目（或项目群）至关重要。常见问题是架构已完成，但实施启动因还需某些额外文档而延迟，战略层与分段层项目尤其如此。

该问题可通过细致的质量管理加以缓解，其依据是与将实施架构的组织单位商定的质量标准（质量管理规划见 3.1 节“定义管理方法”步骤）。TOGAF 标准还强调，实施与迁移计划须与组织内的管理框架协调[10]。

架构项目交付物还可包括新的架构工作请求（将启动新的架构项目）和/或架构能力的变更请求。

架构项目产品的详细映射见图3-3：架构项目最终交付物。



| TOGAF ADM Phases B to F | TOGAF ADM 阶段 B–F |

相关结构化资料：[独立图示解析 fig3-3](../figures/G188/fig3-3.json)。保留当前图示解析、冲突及视觉审校状态。章节翻译不自动授予重绘图视觉通过。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 与实施接收单位确认交付物足以启动实施，并协调其管理框架。
  - 适用边界：文档存在不保证可交接；需接收方质量标准及实际移交记录。
  - PDF 73：sufficient to start the implementation
- 最终交付还可能包含新架构工作请求或架构能力变更请求。
  - 适用边界：可选后续请求不自动启动新项目，也不等同于本次未完成。
  - PDF 73：can also include

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0072:c011 · PDF 72**

3.6.1. Products Handover

**G188:p0072:c012 · PDF 72**

The Architecture Project delivers a definition of an architecture to be implemented. The final step leading to this architecture implementation is handing the deliverables over to the body responsible for implementation.

**G188:p0072:c013 · PDF 72**

At the end of Phase F, the Architecture Project delivers several deliverables that together form the final product. These deliverables, including the Architecture Definition Document, Implementation and Migration Plan, and Architecture Roadmap, should be used as inputs to the program and Project Management activities that are typically executed by another organizational unit (and governed by the

**G188:p0073:c000 · PDF 73**

ADM Phase G).

**G188:p0073:c001 · PDF 73**

Project deliverables should be transferred to the organizational unit that will implement the architecture (e.g., PMO or business owner of the implementation). From our experience, it is crucial to ensure that these deliverables are sufficient to start the implementation project (or program) right away. It is a common issue to have the architecture finished, but the implementation start is delayed due to some additional documentation needed, especially for strategic and segment-level projects.

**G188:p0073:c002 · PDF 73**

This issue can be mitigated with careful quality management based on quality criteria agreed with the organizational unit that will implement the architecture (see the Define the Management Approach step in Section 3.1 for quality management planning). The TOGAF Standard also highlights the need to coordinate the Implementation and Migration Plan with the management frameworks within the

**G188:p0073:c003 · PDF 73**

[10]

**G188:p0073:c004 · PDF 73**

organization.

**G188:p0073:c005 · PDF 73**

Architecture Project deliverables can also include new Requests for Architecture Work (that will start a new Architecture Project) and/or change requests for architecture capability.

**G188:p0073:c006 · PDF 73**

The detailed mapping of Architecture Project products is presented in Figure 3-3.

**G188:p0074:c000 · PDF 74**

Figure 3-3: Architecture Project Final Deliverables

</details>

<a id="3.6.2"></a>
## 准备并建议收尾

原文：3.6.2. Prepare and Recommend Closure。PDF 物理页：[74, 75]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

为正式关闭项目，项目经理须编制收尾报告，并向项目治理机构建议关闭项目。

项目收尾报告应包含在项目各阶段收集的信息，并应总结 [PMBOK]：

- 架构项目交付的产品；
- 工作绩效——项目在时间、成本、质量、范围与收益目标方面的绩效；
- 风险与问题摘要，以及如何缓解；
- 架构能力绩效信息。

报告应提交项目治理机构。

关闭项目需要执行发起人的正式接受，通常在提交项目收尾报告后取得。项目经理还应与执行发起人及利益相关者确认，所有架构项目交付物均已由其评审并接受。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 收尾需执行发起人正式接受，并确认相关方已经评审接受交付物。
  - 适用边界：项目经理建议收尾或写出总结报告尚未构成正式关闭。
  - PDF 75：formal acceptance of the Executive

**G188:p0074:c001 · PDF 74**

3.6.2. Prepare and Recommend Closure

**G188:p0074:c002 · PDF 74**

To close the project formally, the Project Manager has to prepare a closing report and recommend project closure to the project governance.

**G188:p0074:c003 · PDF 74**

The project closing report should contain information collected throughout all stages of the project. The report should summarize [PMBOK]:

**G188:p0074:c004 · PDF 74**

• Products that were delivered by the Architecture Project

**G188:p0074:c005 · PDF 74**

• Work performance — how did the project perform against targets for time, cost, quality, scope, and benefit

**G188:p0074:c006 · PDF 74**

• Risks and issues summary, and how were they mitigated

**G188:p0074:c007 · PDF 74**

• Architecture capability performance information

**G188:p0074:c008 · PDF 74**

The report should be presented to the project governance.

**G188:p0075:c000 · PDF 75**

Closing the project needs formal acceptance of the Executive. This is typically obtained after presenting the project closing report. The Project Manager should also confirm with the Executive and stakeholders that all Architecture Project deliverables are reviewed and accepted by them.

</details>

<a id="3.6.3"></a>
## 评估项目并记录经验教训

原文：3.6.3. Evaluate the Project and Document Lessons Learned。PDF 物理页：[75, 76]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

架构项目的最后一步应是项目评估。团队应记录经验教训，供后续架构项目使用，并提出架构能力改进建议。

从知识管理与架构实践持续改进的角度看，这一步非常重要。经验教训文档应始终被视为后续架构项目的输入。

经验教训应记录关于产品质量与项目绩效数据（如时间与成本）的项目假设，并与项目结果比较。若有偏差，经验教训应包含对影响偏差的因素的分析，以及如何改进架构项目管理实践的推论。

基于经验教训，项目团队可提出架构能力改进建议。按 TOGAF 标准，这些建议应包含将在 ADM 阶段 H（架构变更管理）处理的变更请求。

> **示例 3-37**
>
> 在项目 A 中，项目经理与团队召开了项目评估会议。会上他推荐采用事后回顾（After Action Review, AAR）方法评估项目。会议期间收集了全部与项目绩效相关的信息。
>
> 会议有结构化的议程——在项目经理作开场介绍、说明会议目标之后，团队讨论并回答了以下问题：
>
> - 项目结束时我们本应实现什么？
> - 我们实际实现了什么？
> - 为什么存在差异？
> - 我们学到了什么，未来项目可以改进什么？
>
> 他们使用以下经验教训模板记录了会议结果：
>
> | 序号 | 问题名称 | 问题描述 | 对项目的影响 | 建议 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | | 应用清单的确认耗时超过计划 | 我们在与利益相关者就应用清单达成一致时遇到了问题。耗时一个月，而非 10 天。我们发现这项工作缺乏正式化、系统化的方法。 | 我们必须调整进度并增派资源，以赶上项目截止日期。 | 我们需要为利益相关者准备一套通用模板，以提高其工作效率。 |
> | | CFO 表示其对应用成本的关注未得到妥善处理 | CFO 表示，他希望把应用组合优化可能带来的财务节省作为决策因素加以了解。 | 我们必须修改受 CFO 关注影响的某些视图。 | 我们建议把应用许可费用与维护成本加入应用功能矩阵。 |

**注释**

[1] 当然，两者可以是同一个人。

[2] 本文档中，经验记录（Lessons Log）指项目期间持续填充的仓库，区别于项目结束时编制、仅含最重要信息的经验教训（Lessons Learned）或经验报告（Lessons Report）。

[3] 见 TOGAF 标准——架构开发方法：阶段 F：迁移规划（完成架构开发周期并记录经验教训）以及 TOGAF 标准——架构开发方法：阶段 H：架构变更管理（变革驱动因素）。

[4] 互联网上关于该框架的资源很多，涵盖不同应用；我们未推荐特定资源。

[5] 由于讨论的是架构项目管理，项目治理是双重的——架构治理与项目治理均适用。

[6] 该术语在 PMBOK 中用于描述“项目经理以及共同执行项目工作以实现项目目标的一组人员”。如前所述，该术语与 PRINCE2 的项目管理团队不同：它不包含提供监督的机构（此处称为 Project Owner），而包含全部运营人员——这是另一方法未涵盖的。

[7] PMBOK 使用“phase”一词。本文档在指 PRINCE2 的 “stage” 与 PMBOK 的 “phase” 时使用 “stage” 一词；而 “phase” 按 TOGAF 定义使用（ADM 由若干阶段构成，这些阶段不一定等同于架构项目阶段）。

[8] 关于对 ADM 应用迭代的更多信息，参见 TOGAF 标准——应用 ADM：对 ADM 应用迭代（迭代考虑因素）。

[9] 关于架构开发方式的更多信息，参见 TOGAF 标准——应用 ADM：对 ADM 应用迭代（架构开发方式）。

[10] 见 TOGAF 标准——架构开发方法：阶段 F：迁移规划（步骤）。

相关结构化资料：[本指南完整结构化表格](../tables/G188.json)。保留原表数值、依赖、空值及源文异常；案例不是当前项目事实。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 经验教训比较质量假设、时间成本绩效与实际结果，解释偏差并提出改进。
  - 适用边界：不能只列一般感想；需可追溯计划/实际及影响因素。
  - PDF 75：comparison with project results
- 本节将架构能力改进请求交由阶段H处理。
  - 适用边界：图3-3的A新能力周期另有表述，保持两处来源，不擅自将二者合成唯一执行路线。
  - PDF 75：handled in the ADM Phase H

**G188:p0075:c001 · PDF 75**

3.6.3. Evaluate the Project and Document Lessons Learned

**G188:p0075:c002 · PDF 75**

The final step of an Architecture Project should be its evaluation. The team should document the lessons learned for further Architecture Projects and recommend architecture capability improvements.

**G188:p0075:c003 · PDF 75**

This step is very important from the perspective of knowledge management and continuous improvement of architectural practice. Lessons learned documentation should always be considered as an input to further Architecture Projects.

**G188:p0075:c004 · PDF 75**

Lessons learned should document project assumptions about product quality, project performance data (e.g., time and costs), and comparison with project results. If there are any deviations, lessons learned should contain the analysis of factors that impacted these deviations and deduction of how to improve the Architecture Project management practice.

**G188:p0075:c005 · PDF 75**

Based on lessons learned, the Project Team can define recommendations for architecture capability improvement. According to the TOGAF Standard, these recommendations should include change requests that will be handled in the ADM Phase H (Architecture Change Management).

**G188:p0075:c006 · PDF 75**

Example 3-37

**G188:p0075:c007 · PDF 75**

In Project A, the Project Manager established a project evaluation meeting with his team. During this meeting he recommend an After Action Review (AAR) approach to evaluate the project. During this meeting all project performance-related information was gathered.

**G188:p0075:c008 · PDF 75**

The meeting had a structured agenda — after the Project Manager introduction that explained the meeting goals, the team discussed and answered the following questions:

**G188:p0075:c009 · PDF 75**

• What were we supposed to achieve at the end of the project?

**G188:p0075:c010 · PDF 75**

• What did we achieve?

**G188:p0075:c011 · PDF 75**

• Why is there a difference?

**G188:p0075:c012 · PDF 75**

• What did we learn and what can we improve in our future projects?

**G188:p0075:c013 · PDF 75**

They documented the meeting results using the following lessons learned template:

**G188:p0076:c000 · PDF 76**

|  | No. | Issue Name | Issue Description | Impact on Project | Recommendation |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  |  | Agreement of<br>application list takes<br>more time than<br>planned. | We had a problem with<br>agreement on the list of<br>applications with<br>stakeholders. It took 1<br>month instead of 10 days.<br>We found that there was<br>a lack of formalized and<br>systematic approach to<br>this task. | We have to provide the<br>changes in schedule and<br>assign extra resources to<br>meet the project deadline. | We need to prepare a<br>set of common<br>templates for our<br>stakeholders that will<br>improve the efficiency<br>of their work. |  |
|  |  | The CFO stated that<br>his concerns about<br>the costs of<br>applications were<br>not addressed<br>properly. | The CFO stated that he<br>would like to know<br>potential financial<br>savings on application<br>portfolio optimization as<br>a decision factor. | We have to modify some<br>views affected by CFO<br>concerns. | We recommend adding<br>the cost of the<br>application license and<br>maintenance cost to<br>the application<br>function matrix. |  |

**G188:p0076:c001 · PDF 76**

Endnotes

**G188:p0076:c002 · PDF 76**

[1] The two can be, of course, the same person. [2] In this document we refer to the Lessons Log as a repository filled continuously during the project, as opposed to Lessons Learned or a Lessons Report, which are the documents concluding the project and containing only the most important information. [3] The TOGAF Standard — Architecture Development Method: Phase F: Migration Planning (Complete the Architecture Development Cycle and Document Lessons Learned) and the TOGAF Standard — Architecture Development Method: Phase H: Architecture Change Management (Drivers for Change). [4] There is multitude of resources on this framework on the Internet, covering different applications; we have not recommended any particular resource. [5] Since we are discussing Architecture Project Management, the governance of the project is two-fold — both architecture governance and project governance are applicable. [6] This term is used in the PMBOK to describe the “Project Manager and the group of individuals who act together in performing the work of the project to achieve its objectives”. As mentioned earlier, this term differs from the Project Management Team used in PRINCE2, because it does not include the body providing oversight (herein called the Project Owner), as opposed to the Project Management Team, but includes all the operational people – something which is not covered in the other method. [7] The PMBOK uses the word “phase”. In this document, we use the word “stage” when referring to PRINCE2 “stages” and PMBOK “phases”. We use the word “phase” according to its TOGAF definition (the ADM consists of phases, which do not necessarily equal Architecture Project stages). [8] Refer to the TOGAF Standard — Applying the ADM: Applying Iteration to the ADM (Iteration Considerations) for more information on applying iteration to the ADM. [9] Refer to the TOGAF Standard — Applying the ADM: Applying Iteration to the ADM (Approaches to Architecture Development) for more information on approaches to architecture development. [10] The TOGAF Standard — Architecture Development Method: Phase F: Migration Planning (Steps).

</details>

<a id="4"></a>
## PRINCE2 到 ADM 的映射

原文：4. PRINCE2 to ADM Mapping。PDF 物理页：[77]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本节旨在给出 TOGAF ADM [C220] 与 PRINCE2 [PRINCE2] 的映射。本节提供的是关于映射的一般信息，而非 TOGAF ADM 阶段与 PRINCE2 流程的详细信息，因此标准术语直接使用，不作解释。更详细的信息请参阅 TOGAF 标准与《Managing Successful Projects with PRINCE2》。

一般而言，TOGAF ADM 阶段 A（架构愿景）可映射到 PRINCE2 的“项目启动（Starting up the project）”与“发起（Initiation）”流程。TOGAF ADM 阶段 B（业务架构）、阶段 C（信息系统架构）、阶段 D（技术架构）、阶段 E（机会与解决方案）、阶段 F（迁移规划）可归为一组，视为实质性的架构项目阶段——按 PRINCE2，应视为工作交付阶段（work delivery stages）。

把 TOGAF ADM 阶段 B–F 划分为 PRINCE2 工作交付阶段，取决于架构项目的规模与约束。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- B–F映射到工作交付阶段的划分取决于项目规模与约束。
  - 适用边界：映射不是每个ADM阶段固定创建一个独立管理阶段。
  - PDF 77：depends on the scale and constraints

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0077:c000 · PDF 77**

4. PRINCE2 to ADM Mapping

**G188:p0077:c001 · PDF 77**

The aim of this section is to present a mapping of the TOGAF ADM [C220] to PRINCE2 [PRINCE2]. This section is assumed to provide general information about the mapping, not detailed information about the TOGAF ADM phases and PRINCE2 processes; therefore, the standard terms are used without description. For more detailed information, please refer to the TOGAF Standard and Managing Successful Projects with PRINCE2.

**G188:p0077:c002 · PDF 77**

In general, the TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision) can be mapped to the PRINCE2 processes “Starting up the project” and “Initiation”. The TOGAF ADM Phase B (Business Architecture), Phase C (Information Systems Architectures), Phase D (Technology Architecture), Phase E (Opportunities and Solutions), and Phase F (Migration Planning) can be grouped together and considered substantive Architecture Project phases, which – according to PRINCE2 – should be considered work delivery stages.

**G188:p0077:c003 · PDF 77**

Division of the TOGAF ADM Phase B to F into PRINCE2 work delivery stages depends on the scale and constraints of the Architecture Project.

</details>

| Recommend Closure | 建议收尾 |

- 高层映射归并简化任务且只显示主要交付物。
  - 适用边界：图中缺少某制品不证明该制品不存在或可以省略。
  - PDF 77：aggregated and simplified

4.1. High-Level Mapping Description

**G188:p0077:c005 · PDF 77**

Figure 4-1 presents the results of the TOGAF ADM mapping into PRINCE2 Project Management processes at a high level. In order to ensure clear understanding of the mapping, some tasks and processes were aggregated and simplified. Furthermore, only the main deliverables are presented.

<a id="4.1.1"></a>
## TOGAF ADM 阶段 A（架构愿景）

原文：4.1.1. TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision)。PDF 物理页：[77, 78]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

图 4-1 应自上而下阅读，从阶段 A 的第一个 ADM 任务“建立架构项目”开始。在该 TOGAF 任务中，架构活动应使用企业公认的实践来规划与管理。

为了在“建立架构项目”期间用 PRINCE2 方法管理架构项目，建议执行 PRINCE2“项目启动”阶段的以下任务：

- 任命项目执行发起人与项目经理；
- 设计项目管理团队；
- 编制概要商业论证；
- 选择项目方法并汇编（原文此处为“Select Project Approach & Assemble”，未写明汇编对象）；
- 规划发起阶段。

阶段计划（发起阶段）是“项目启动”阶段的成果，并视为后续 TOGAF ADM 阶段 A 任务与 PRINCE2“发起”阶段的输入，二者应并行开展。以下 ADM 阶段 A 任务：

- 识别利益相关者、关注点与业务需求；
- 确认并细化业务目标、业务驱动因素与约束；
- 评估能力；
- 评估业务转型就绪度；
- 定义范围；
- 确认并细化架构原则（含业务原则）；
- 制定架构愿景；
- 定义目标架构价值主张与 KPI；
- 识别业务转型风险与缓解活动；
- 编制架构工作说明书；获得批准

应与 PRINCE2“发起”阶段的以下任务并行执行：

- 编制管理策略、商业论证与项目控制；
- 创建项目计划；
- 细化商业论证。

请注意，部分 TOGAF ADM 阶段 A 任务产出项目管理交付物，这些交付物在图 4-1 中以虚线表示用于 PRINCE2 流程。ADM 阶段 A 的最后一个任务是架构工作说明书的定义与批准；该说明书连同交付物应纳入项目启动文档（Project Initiation Document），后者是 PRINCE2“发起”阶段的主要交付物，在最后一个流程（汇编项目启动文档）中形成。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 经批准的架构工作说明书及相关交付物纳入项目启动文档。
  - 适用边界：容器关系不表示两份文档同义或审批主体完全相同。
  - PDF 78：should be included in the Project Initiation Document

**G188:p0077:c006 · PDF 77**

4.1.1. TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision)

**G188:p0077:c007 · PDF 77**

Figure 4-1 should be read from top to bottom starting from the first task of the ADM in Phase A: Establish the Architecture Project. In this TOGAF task architecture activities should be planned and managed using accepted practices for the enterprise.

**G188:p0077:c008 · PDF 77**

In order to manage the Architecture Project using the PRINCE2 method during “Establish the Architecture Project”, it is recommended to execute following tasks from the PRINCE2 “Starting up the project” stage:

**G188:p0077:c009 · PDF 77**

• Appoint Project Executives and Project Manager

**G188:p0077:c010 · PDF 77**

• Design a Project Management Team

**G188:p0077:c011 · PDF 77**

• Prepare Outline Business Case

**G188:p0077:c012 · PDF 77**

• Select Project Approach & Assemble

**G188:p0077:c013 · PDF 77**

• Plan the Initiation Stage

**G188:p0078:c000 · PDF 78**

The Stage Plan (initiation) is the result of the “Starting up the project” stage and is considered an input to the next TOGAF ADM Phase A tasks and PRINCE2 “Initiation” stage, which should be performed in parallel. The following ADM Phase A tasks:

**G188:p0078:c001 · PDF 78**

• Identify Stakeholders, Concerns, and Business Requirements

**G188:p0078:c002 · PDF 78**

• Confirm and Elaborate Business Goals, Business Drivers, and Constraints

**G188:p0078:c003 · PDF 78**

• Evaluate Capabilities

**G188:p0078:c004 · PDF 78**

• Assess Readiness for Business Transformation

**G188:p0078:c005 · PDF 78**

• Define Scope

**G188:p0078:c006 · PDF 78**

• Confirm and Elaborate Architecture Principles, including Business Principles

**G188:p0078:c007 · PDF 78**

• Develop Architecture Vision

**G188:p0078:c008 · PDF 78**

• Define the Target Architecture Value Propositions and KPIs

**G188:p0078:c009 · PDF 78**

• Identify the Business Transformation Risks and Mitigation Activities

**G188:p0078:c010 · PDF 78**

• Develop Statement of Architecture Work; Secure Approval

**G188:p0078:c011 · PDF 78**

should be performed in parallel with the following PRINCE2 “Initiation” stage tasks:

**G188:p0078:c012 · PDF 78**

• Prepare Management Strategy, Business Case, and Project Controls

**G188:p0078:c013 · PDF 78**

• Create Project Plan

**G188:p0078:c014 · PDF 78**

• Refine the Business Case

**G188:p0078:c015 · PDF 78**

Be aware that some of the TOGAF ADM Phase A tasks produce Project Management deliverables which are used in PRINCE2 processes as shown in Figure 4-1 with a dotted line. The final task in ADM Phase A is the Statement of Architecture Work Definition and Approval, which along with deliverables should be included in the Project Initiation Document, which is the main deliverable from the PRINCE2 “Initiation” stage assembled in the last process (Assemble the Project Initiation Document).

</details>

<a id="4.2.1"></a>
## TOGAF ADM 阶段 A（架构愿景）

原文：4.2.1. TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision)。PDF 物理页：[80, 81, 82]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

以下流程/阶段经过细化，以精确反映 TOGAF ADM 与 PRINCE2。

ADM 阶段 A 的全部任务连同主要交付物均纳入详细映射：

- **建立架构项目**——作为 PRINCE2“项目启动”阶段的输入；
- **识别利益相关者、关注点与业务需求**——该任务的结果应在 PRINCE2“建立项目控制”流程中使用；
- **确认并细化业务目标、业务驱动因素与约束**——产出细化的业务原则、目标与驱动因素陈述；
- **评估能力**；
- **评估业务转型就绪度**——产出能力评估（Capability Assessment）；
- **定义范围**——影响 PRINCE2“细化商业论证”流程；
- **确认并细化架构原则（含业务原则）**——产出架构原则；
- **制定架构愿景**——产出架构定义文档草案与架构愿景，二者是 PRINCE2“创建项目计划”流程的输入；
- **定义目标架构价值主张与 KPI**；
- **识别业务转型风险与缓解活动**——产出沟通计划；
- **编制架构工作说明书；获得批准**——产出经批准的架构工作说明书，应成为 PRINCE2 项目启动文档的一部分，后者在 PRINCE2“汇编项目启动文档”流程中完成。

PRINCE2“项目启动”阶段的全部流程连同全部交付物均纳入详细映射：

- **任命执行发起人与项目经理**——应在 ADM“建立架构项目”任务之后开始，产出项目管理团队角色描述（项目任务书的一部分）；
- **汲取以往经验**——产出经验记录（Lessons Log）；
- **编制概要商业论证**——产出概要商业论证与项目产品描述（均为项目任务书的一部分）；
- **设计并任命项目管理团队**——产出项目管理团队结构与更新的团队角色描述（均为项目任务书的一部分）；
- **选择项目方法并汇编项目任务书**——产出项目方法（项目任务书的一部分）；
- **规划发起阶段**——产出发起阶段计划。

PRINCE2“发起”阶段的全部流程连同全部交付物均纳入详细映射。

项目管理策略的编制（可并行执行）：

- **编制风险管理策略**——产出风险管理策略（项目启动文档的一部分）与风险登记册；
- **编制质量管理策略**——产出质量管理策略（项目启动文档的一部分）与质量登记册；
- **编制配置管理策略**——产出配置管理策略（项目启动文档的一部分）、配置项记录与问题登记册；
- **编制沟通管理策略**——产出沟通管理策略（项目启动文档的一部分）。

项目计划与项目控制的编制（可在项目管理策略完成后并行执行）：

- **建立项目控制**——产出项目控制（项目启动文档的一部分）。ADM 步骤“识别利益相关者、关注点与业务需求”的结果应在该流程中使用，以确保项目控制机制的完整性；
- **创建项目计划**——产出项目计划（项目启动文档的一部分）。在“制定架构愿景”中编制的架构定义文档草案应视为架构项目全部活动规划的输入。

在先前“发起”阶段各流程完成后，应按顺序执行以下任务：

- **细化商业论证**——产出详细商业论证（项目启动文档的一部分）与收益评审计划。在此流程中应考虑 ADM 步骤“定义范围”的结果；
- **汇编项目启动文档**——产出项目启动文档。经批准的架构工作说明书（来自 ADM 步骤“编制架构工作说明书；获得批准”）应纳入项目启动文档。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 风险/质量/配置/沟通策略可并行；完成后项目计划与控制可并行。
  - 适用边界：并行性带有前置条件，不是所有阶段A与PRINCE2流程无条件一起开始。
  - PDF 81：after completion of the Project Management Strategies
- 随后细化商业论证并汇编启动文档，具有顺序约束。
  - 适用边界：不能将来源步骤清单当无序制品集合。
  - PDF 81：sequentially

4.2.1. TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision)

**G188:p0080:c003 · PDF 80**

The following processes/stages were detailed to exactly reflect the TOGAF ADM and PRINCE2.

**G188:p0080:c004 · PDF 80**

All of the ADM Phase A tasks were included in the detailed mapping, along with the main deliverables:

**G188:p0080:c005 · PDF 80**

Establish the Architecture Project, which is input to the PRINCE2 “Starting up the project” stage

**G188:p0080:c006 · PDF 80**

Identify Stakeholders, Concerns, and Business Requirements — results from this task should be used during PRINCE2 “Set up the project controls” process

**G188:p0080:c007 · PDF 80**

• Confirm and Elaborate Business Goals, Business Drivers, and Constraints — resulting in refined statements of business principles, goals, and drivers

**G188:p0080:c008 · PDF 80**

• Evaluate Capabilities

**G188:p0080:c009 · PDF 80**

• Assess Readiness for Business Transformation — resulting in a Capability Assessment

**G188:p0080:c010 · PDF 80**

• Define Scope, which has an impact on the PRINCE2 “Refine the Business Case” process

**G188:p0080:c011 · PDF 80**

• Confirm and Elaborate Architecture Principles, including Business Principles — resulting in Architecture Principles

**G188:p0080:c012 · PDF 80**

• Develop Architecture Vision — resulting in a Draft Architecture Definition Document and Architecture Vision which are inputs to the PRINCE2 “Create the Project Plan” process

**G188:p0080:c013 · PDF 80**

• Define the Target Architecture Value Propositions and KPIs

**G188:p0080:c014 · PDF 80**

• Identify the Business Transformation Risks and Mitigation Activities — resulting in a Communications Plan

**G188:p0080:c015 · PDF 80**

• Develop Statement of Architecture Work; Secure Approval — resulting in an Approved Statement of Architecture Work, which should be part of the PRINCE2 Project Initiation Documentation that is to be completed during the PRINCE2 “Assemble the Project Initiation Documentation” process

**G188:p0080:c016 · PDF 80**

All of the PRINCE2 “Starting up the project” processes were included in the detailed mapping, along with all deliverables:

**G188:p0080:c017 · PDF 80**

• Appoint the Executive & Project Manager, which should be started after the “Establish the Architecture Project” task from the ADM, resulting in the Project Management team role descriptions (part of the Project Brief)

**G188:p0080:c018 · PDF 80**

• Capture previous lessons — resulting in a Lessons Log

**G188:p0081:c000 · PDF 81**

• Prepare the outline Business Case — resulting in the Outline Business Case and Project Product Description (both part of the Project Brief)

**G188:p0081:c001 · PDF 81**

• Design and appoint the Project Management team — resulting in the Project Management team structure and updated Project Management team role descriptions (both part of the Project Brief)

**G188:p0081:c002 · PDF 81**

• Select the project approach & assemble the Project Brief — resulting in the Project Approach (part of the Project Brief)

**G188:p0081:c003 · PDF 81**

• Plan the initiation stage — resulting in the Initiation stage plan

**G188:p0081:c004 · PDF 81**

All of the PRINCE2 “Initiation” processes were included in the detailed mapping, along with all deliverables.

**G188:p0081:c005 · PDF 81**

Development of Project Management Strategies, that can be executed in parallel:

**G188:p0081:c006 · PDF 81**

• Prepare the Risk Management Strategy — resulting in the Risk Management Strategy (part of the Project Initiation Documentation) and Risk Register

**G188:p0081:c007 · PDF 81**

• Prepare the Quality Management Strategy — resulting in the Quality Management Strategy (part of the Project Initiation Documentation) and Quality Register

**G188:p0081:c008 · PDF 81**

• Prepare Configuration Management Strategy — resulting in the Configuration Management Strategy (part of the Project Initiation Documentation), Configuration Item Records, and Issue Register

**G188:p0081:c009 · PDF 81**

• Prepare the Communication Management Strategy — resulting in the Communication Management Strategy (part of the Project Initiation Documentation)

**G188:p0081:c010 · PDF 81**

Development of Project Plan and project controls, that can be executed in parallel after completion of the Project Management Strategies:

**G188:p0081:c011 · PDF 81**

• Set up the project controls — resulting in the Project Controls (part of the Project Initiation Documentation)

**G188:p0081:c012 · PDF 81**

Results from the ADM step Identify Stakeholders, Concerns, and Business Requirements should be used during this process to ensure the integrity of the project control mechanisms.

**G188:p0081:c013 · PDF 81**

• Create the Project Plan — resulting in the Project Plan (part of the Project Initiation Documentation)

**G188:p0081:c014 · PDF 81**

The Draft Architecture Definition Document prepared during Develop Architecture Vision should be considered as input to planning all activities during the Architecture Project.

**G188:p0081:c015 · PDF 81**

The following tasks should be executed sequentially after completion of the previous “Initiation” stage processes:

**G188:p0081:c016 · PDF 81**

• Refine the Business Case — resulting in the Detailed Business Case (part of the Project Initiation Documentation) and Benefits Review Plan

**G188:p0081:c017 · PDF 81**

Results from the ADM step Define Scope should be considered during this process.

**G188:p0082:c000 · PDF 82**

• Assemble the Project Initiation Documentation — resulting in the Project Initiation Documentation

**G188:p0082:c001 · PDF 82**

The approved Statement of Architecture Work (resulting from the ADM step Develop Statement of Architecture Work; Secure Approval) should be included in the Project Initiation Document.

## TOGAF ADM 阶段 B–F

原文：4.2.2. TOGAF ADM Phases B to F。PDF 物理页：[82, 83]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

如第 2.3 节所述（原文如此；就阶段定位而言亦见 4.1.2），TOGAF ADM 阶段 B–F 视为 PRINCE2 工作交付阶段，应在 PRINCE2“管理阶段边界”下规划，在“控制阶段”下控制，在“管理产品交付”下交付。阶段 B–F 未规定任何项目管理活动，因此应按 PRINCE2 方法管理。

图4-2：TOGAF ADM与PRINCE2映射（详细）（据TOGAF标准及PRINCE2）。

相关结构化资料：[独立图示解析 fig4-2](../figures/G188/fig4-2.json)。保留当前图示解析、冲突及视觉审校状态。章节翻译不自动授予重绘图视觉通过。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 本节认为B–F专业交付应由PRINCE2补充管理。
  - 适用边界：这是映射语境；不是抹去B–F中正式评审与F的收尾等实际活动。
  - PDF 82：do not specify any Project Management activities

As described in Section 2.3, the TOGAF ADM Phases B to F are considered as PRINCE2 work delivery stages and should be planned under the PRINCE2 Managing Stage Boundary, controlled under the PRINCE2 “Controlling the stage”, and delivered under the PRINCE2 Managing Product Delivery. Phases B to F do not specify any Project Management activities; therefore, should be managed according to the PRINCE2 method.

<a id="5"></a>
## PMBOK 到 ADM 的映射

原文：5. PMBOK to ADM Mapping。PDF 物理页：[84, 85, 86]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本节旨在给出 TOGAF ADM [C220] 与 PMBOK 流程 [PMBOK] 的映射。本节意在提供关于映射的一般信息，而非 TOGAF ADM 阶段与 PMBOK 流程的详细信息；因此标准术语直接使用，不作解释。详细说明见 TOGAF 标准与 PMBOK® 指南第 5 版。

一般而言，TOGAF ADM 阶段 A（架构愿景）可映射到 PMBOK 的启动与规划过程组。TOGAF ADM 阶段 B（业务架构）、阶段 C（信息系统架构）、阶段 D（技术架构）、阶段 E（机会与解决方案）与阶段 F（迁移规划）可归为一组，视为交付阶段。项目收尾应视为阶段 F 的最后一步。

TOGAF 标准提供的 ADM 定义了企业架构生命周期。ADM 中不同阶段又划分为步骤，描述与架构规划、开发、实施及架构变更管理相关的流程。

PMBOK 把流程划分为知识领域与过程组。知识领域包括：整合管理、范围管理、时间管理、成本管理、质量管理、人力资源管理、沟通管理、风险管理、采购管理与利益相关者管理。过程组分为：启动、规划、执行、监控与收尾。

TOGAF标准建议把ADM周期作为项目管理，并采用企业已实施的项目管理框架；参见TOGAF标准——架构开发方法：预备阶段：方法（管理框架及管理框架之间的关系）。基于 ADM 阶段与项目管理过程组，我们可以初步把 PMBOK 项目管理流程映射到 TOGAF ADM。

以下给出项目管理流程映射到 ADM 步骤的详细说明。流程活动在 TOGAF ADM 步骤中被全部或部分覆盖；有些流程 TOGAF 标准未涵盖，我们将其作为附加的项目管理流程讨论。

图 5-1 呈现 TOGAF ADM 与 PMBOK 流程的高层映射。为确保清晰理解，部分任务与流程作了归并和简化。”。）



| Close Project or Phase | 结束项目或阶段 |

图5-1：TOGAF ADM与PMBOK映射（高层）（据TOGAF标准及PMBOK指南）。

本节以下部分详细描述 PMBOK 流程到 TOGAF ADM 周期的映射。首先评估阶段 A，把每个步骤映射到 PMBOK 流程；然后评估阶段 B–F；最后呈现架构项目的收尾部分。映射包括解释为何以及如何把特定流程映射到各步骤。

相关结构化资料：[独立图示解析 fig5-1](../figures/G188/fig5-1.json)。保留当前图示解析、冲突及视觉审校状态。章节翻译不自动授予重绘图视觉通过。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 本章映射的明确版本是PMBOK第5版。
  - 适用边界：不能静默升级到较新版本或把源编号视为当前全部版本通用编号。
  - PDF 84：PMBOK Guide, 5th Edition

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G188:p0084:c000 · PDF 84**

5. PMBOK to ADM Mapping

**G188:p0084:c001 · PDF 84**

The aim of this section is to present the TOGAF ADM mapping [C220] to PMBOK processes [PMBOK]. This section is intended to provide general information about the mapping, not detailed information about the TOGAF ADM phases and PMBOK processes; therefore, the standard terms are used without description. A detailed description can be seen in the TOGAF Standard and the PMBOK Guide, 5th Edition.

**G188:p0084:c002 · PDF 84**

In general, the TOGAF ADM Phase A (Architecture Vision) can be mapped to the PMBOK initiation and planning process groups. The TOGAF ADM Phase B (Business Architecture), Phase C (Information Systems Architectures), Phase D (Technology Architecture), Phase E (Opportunities and Solutions), and Phase F (Migration Planning) can be grouped together and considered delivery stages. Project closing should be considered as the last step of Phase F.

**G188:p0084:c003 · PDF 84**

The TOGAF Standard provides the ADM that defines the Enterprise Architecture lifecycle. In this ADM there are distinct phases that are divided into steps that describe the processes related to architecture planning, development, implementation, and architecture change management.

**G188:p0084:c004 · PDF 84**

PMBOK describes the processes divided into knowledge areas and process groups. The knowledge areas contain: integration management, scope management, time management, cost management, quality management, human resource management, communication management, risk management, procurement management, and stakeholder management. The process groups are divided into: initiating, planning, executing, monitoring, and closing.

**G188:p0084:c005 · PDF 84**

The TOGAF Standard recommends managing the ADM cycle as a project and using for this purpose the Project Management framework that is implemented in the enterprise; see [TOGAF Standard — Architecture Development Method: Preliminary Phase: Approach (Management Frameworks, and Relating the Management Frameworks). Based on the ADM phases and Project Management process groups we could initially map the PMBOK Project Management processes into the TOGAF ADM.

**G188:p0084:c006 · PDF 84**

A detailed description of the mapping of the Project Management processes to the ADM steps follows. Process activities are covered fully or partially in the TOGAF ADM steps. Some processes are not covered in the TOGAF Standard so we discuss them as additional Project Management processes.

**G188:p0084:c007 · PDF 84**

Figure 5-1 presents the high-level mapping between the TOGAF ADM and PMBOK processes. In order to ensure clear understanding of mapping, some tasks and processes were aggregated and simplified.

**G188:p0085:c000 · PDF 85**

Figure 5-1: TOGAF ADM and PMBOK Mapping (High-Level) (Derived from the TOGAF Standard and the

**G188:p0085:c001 · PDF 85**

PMBOK Guide)

**G188:p0086:c000 · PDF 86**

The following part of this section presents the detailed description of the PMBOK processes mapping to the TOGAF ADM cycle. First, Phase A is being assessed. In this section each step is mapped to the PMBOK processes. Then, Phases B to F are assessed. The last part presents the closing part of the Architecture Project. The mapping covers an explanation of why and how specific processes are mapped to each of the steps.

</details>
